with a second exit at t8 -- observed while the evidence was already absent -- the same held for an observation captured at t6. **Fix -- the loss is a fact in its own right.** `StaleHookSession` gains `last_loss_observed_at: Option<Instant>` beside the evidence, and the two operations on that record move onto the record itself. `observe_process_loss` advances the boundary on every loss newer than it, whether or not there is any evidence left to expire, and expires the evidence only when the loss is strictly newer than it, so the older-exit control from the row above is unchanged. `record_fresh_process_evidence` becomes a method that refuses any observation not strictly newer than that boundary before applying the existing keep-the-newest rule, so BOTH re-arm paths -- the already-stale loop and the existing-entry branch of `remember_stale_hook_session` -- are gated by one implementation rather than by the absence of evidence. `expire_stale_session_evidence_on_process_loss` keeps the same two limbs and the same call site on every detection observation; it now evaluates the loss predicate first and delegates the record-keeping. **Not weakened.** The boundary bans nothing permanently: a running observation genuinely newer than the latest loss re-arms the session and the explicit same-session resume is admitted exactly as before, which the ported probes assert directly. `explicit_session_start_reclaims_stale_session` still demands both limbs -- a session-start reason this owner starts on AND fresh evidence; nothing reads `effective_agent_label`/`detected_agent` for identity (the loss predicate consults the stale entry's own recorded label against the observation); the retirement funnel `suppress_hook_report_with_session_ref` still scopes evidence to the LATEST retirement; no app-side file changed. **Tests.** The reviewer's two probes (`codex-m3-resume-observation-order-5ffafed.patch`), renamed without the `review_` prefix: `reordered_running_observation_cannot_undo_a_later_exit` and `reordered_running_observation_cannot_undo_a_second_exit` were both RED on `5ffafed` ("a running observation older than the latest exit re-armed the retired session") and are green here. Each also requires a genuinely newer running observation to re-admit the same-session resume, so the boundary cannot degenerate into a permanent ban, and the second drives its stale observation past a loss observed while the evidence was already absent -- the half a boundary tied to the evidence cannot express. Every positive, negative and reporter-shape control from the four rows above stays green unchanged, including `identity_same_session_resume_keeps_process_evidence_after_an_older_exit_observation`, `identity_same_session_resume_cannot_use_process_evidence_invalidated_by_a_later_exit`, `identity_same_session_resume_after_an_invalidating_exit_needs_new_process_evidence`, both same-session resume positives, all three retirement negatives, `identity_late_callback_after_fresh_process_evidence_stays_retired`, both full-lifecycle stale-session generation tests (`stale_session_suppression_survives_multiple_release_generations`, `stale_session_suppression_survives_multiple_process_generations`) and `tests/zynk_receipt.rs::a_bare_same_session_resume_must_not_receipt_for_a_retired_session`. `NOTICE` unchanged (fork-authored, no upstream commit). |
| test-only follow-up: deterministic fake-hermes start | `tests/zynk_receipt.rs` | **The identity-only receipt tests now start their fake `hermes` the way a real agent starts -- as a forked child in its OWN foreground process group -- behind a ready marker with bounded resends.** **Corrected cause.** The `test-only follow-up to 920e1e1` row under the M4 heading below blamed detection latency and raised the deadline from 10 s to 30 s; that attribution was wrong and the raised deadline changed nothing (4 of 20 full parallel `--test zynk_receipt --test zynk_native_cli` runs still failed here, each burning the whole 30 s). `should_probe_foreground_job` (`src/pane.rs`) re-probes a pane that has no agent yet ONLY when the foreground PROCESS GROUP changes, or while the content-driven acquisition window (`PROCESS_ACQUISITION_WINDOW`, 8 s from the pane's first output, rechecked every 500 ms then every 2 s) is still open. The old command `exec bash -c 'exec -a hermes cat'` replaced the pane shell IN PLACE, so the process group never changed, and the silent `cat` then emitted nothing that could reopen the window (`sync_content_change_acquisition` restarts it only on new content). When the injected command landed after the window's last recheck, the pane was never probed again and detection could never report `hermes` -- permanently stuck, not slow, so no deadline could have fixed it. Captured at the deadline: `ps` showed the `hermes`-named `cat` alive as its own session leader AND the tty's foreground process group (`Ss+`, `tpgid == pgid`) while `pane.get` still carried no `agent` at all. **Fix (test-only).** Drop the leading `exec` so the fake agent is a forked child with its own foreground process group, which forces the probe on the next tick regardless of the acquisition window -- and which is also the shape a real agent typed at a prompt has. Keep the `bash -c 'exec -a hermes cat'` layer (argv[0] = `hermes` is what `identify_agent_in_job` reads) without assuming the pane's own shell implements `exec -a`. **Ready marker.** A new shared helper `run_in_pane_until_ready` sends the command, waits up to 10 s for a quote-split `printf` marker emitted just before the agent starts, and resends up to 3 times (each resend led by a newline so a half-delivered line cannot be glued to the front); `start_passive_cat` was refactored onto it and gains the retry. The marker covers the other, real race in this class -- a shell still initializing its line editor discards queued tty input, so a `pane run` can vanish with an `ok` result -- and, by splitting the two stages, it is what proved the command had ALWAYS run and moved the investigation onto the probe gate. Both stages now fail with their own message naming which one gave up. The 30 s detection deadline is kept as headroom. **Evidence.** Before: 4/20 parallel suite runs failed (33-35 s each). After: 25/25 runs at 44/44 (~3 s each, no outliers), 12 solo runs each of `a_same_label_identity_only_pane_with_another_session_cannot_receipt` and `both_report_shapes_bind_the_addressed_session_only`, full suite 2931/2931, `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean. **No production change** -- the probe-gate behaviour above is left exactly as it is; an agent that `exec`s in place over the pane shell after the acquisition window has closed is not currently re-probed, which is recorded here as an observation, not a fix. `NOTICE` unchanged (fork-authored, no upstream commit). |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only fix on top of the `5ffafed`/`d03c717` rows above)* | **The identity retirement boundary becomes the time the observation was CAPTURED, and a process loss seen while an owner is still only suppressed is carried into the stale session it becomes.** Gate-3 B1: warden `WARDEN-R13-OBSERVED-AT-001` (REQUEST_CHANGES on `18c316d..d03c717`, major) plus the arbiter's preliminary probes (`msg_e524c27dd0759d82`). Three defects in one boundary, all reachable through the production state methods. **(1) Capture time vs handler time.** The detector stamps every screen observation at capture time; `publish_state_changed_event` (`src/pane.rs`) preserves that stamp into `AppEvent::StateChanged` and `src/app/actions.rs` passes it unchanged to `set_detected_state_with_screen_signals_at`. On a process exit, however, `suppress_hook_report_with_session_ref` (`src/terminal/state.rs`) wrote `Instant::now()` — the moment the App HANDLED the exit. Handling routinely lags capture: the state channel holds 256 events and each drain is bounded at 64, so a queued burst is an intended execution mode, not an anomaly. A Hermes observation captured at t1 AFTER an exit captured at t0, but handled at h > t1, then satisfied `t1 <= h` in `detected_state_observed_before_release_suppression` and returned BEFORE `clear_hook_suppression_for_detected_agent` ever ran. The retired session therefore never gained fresh process evidence, and the agent's explicit same-session resume was refused — an availability failure that withholds receipt authority from a legitimately resumed owner. **(2) The boundary was lost during the suppression phase.** A retirement lands first in `suppressed_hook_reports` and becomes a `StaleHookSession` only when a running observation converts it. `expire_stale_session_evidence_on_process_loss` iterated the stale sessions alone, so a loss observed during that window — the owner's own exit, or a different agent detected in its place — was recorded nowhere; the session the conversion then created started with `last_loss_observed_at: None`, and a running observation captured BEFORE that loss re-armed it. **(3) Equality.** `observe_process_loss` advanced the boundary at equality (`lost_at < observed_at`) but expired evidence only when strictly older (`recorded_at < observed_at`), so evidence recorded at the loss's own instant survived the loss — the documented rule for a re-arm is strictly-newer on both sides. **Fix.** `observed_at` is threaded through the whole retirement funnel — `retire_hook_identity`, `suppress_current_hook_authority`, `suppress_hook_report`, `suppress_hook_report_with_session_ref` — into `SuppressedHookReport.observed_at`. Every detection call site passes the detector's `now`; handler time survives only where there IS no observation, namely `clear_hook_authority_with_mutation` and `release_agent_with_mutation`, which pass their own `now`. `SuppressedHookReport` gains `last_loss_observed_at: Option<Instant>`, seeded by a `ProcessExit` retirement (that observation IS a loss) and advanced by `observe_process_loss_for_retired_owners` — the renamed loss pass, which now evaluates the same two-limb predicate over the suppressed reports and the stale sessions alike. Both conversions out of the suppression window carry that boundary through: `clear_hook_suppression_for_detected_agent` and `retire_suppressed_session_after_accepting`. `remember_stale_hook_session` applies the carried loss BEFORE the evidence, through `StaleHookSession`'s own accessors, so the very observation that performs the conversion is gated by the boundary it inherits. `StaleHookSession::observe_process_loss` expires evidence at `<=`, and the reclaim gate reads a new `has_reclaimable_process_evidence` (evidence strictly newer than the retained boundary) instead of `fresh_process_evidence.is_some()`, so the two sides cannot drift apart. **Not weakened.** No retirement negative is relaxed: `explicit_session_start_reclaims_stale_session` still demands both limbs — a session-start reason this owner starts on AND fresh evidence; nothing reads `effective_agent_label`/`detected_agent` for identity (both loss predicates consult the retired entry's own recorded label); the funnel still scopes evidence to the LATEST retirement; a capture-time boundary is a STRICTER fence than handler time for the pre-exit case, which the new negatives assert directly. No production file outside `src/terminal/state.rs` changed — `src/pane.rs` and `src/app/actions.rs` already carried `observed_at` end to end, which the new App-level test proves. **Tests.** Six new, all RED on `d03c717` and green here. The arbiter's three probes, renamed without the `review_` prefix and extended with a re-arm tail: `queued_running_observation_newer_than_its_exit_outlives_handler_delay` ("a running observation captured after the exit was discarded solely because the app handled the exit later"), `process_loss_while_suppressed_fences_an_older_running_observation` ("a running observation older than a loss seen while suppression was pending re-armed the retired session") and `equal_time_process_loss_invalidates_resume_evidence` ("process-loss evidence at the same timestamp left stale resume evidence armed"). Plus `queued_running_observation_older_than_its_exit_stays_retired` (the pre-exit and equal-time negatives, then a genuinely newer capture that must still re-arm), `a_different_agent_seen_while_suppressed_fences_an_older_running_observation` (the second limb of a loss across the same window) and `app::actions::tests::queued_state_events_keep_a_running_observation_newer_than_its_exit`, which drives the whole `AppEvent::StateChanged` -> `AgentSessionReported` path so the capture stamps are exercised where the finding located them. Every existing identity, evidence and retirement control stays green unchanged, including `identity_same_session_resume_keeps_process_evidence_after_an_older_exit_observation`, `identity_same_session_resume_cannot_use_process_evidence_invalidated_by_a_later_exit`, `identity_same_session_resume_after_an_invalidating_exit_needs_new_process_evidence`, both `reordered_running_observation_cannot_undo_*`, both same-session resume positives, all three retirement negatives and `identity_late_callback_after_fresh_process_evidence_stays_retired`. Verified in an isolated runtime and target: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings`, `terminal::state`+`app::` 728/728 (722 before), `--test zynk_receipt --test zynk_native_cli` 44/44 three times, full `--bin zynk` 2627/2627. `NOTICE` unchanged (fork-authored, no upstream commit). |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only fix on top of the `26e83e3` row above)* | **A retirement that observes no process — an API release or clear — is retained as a boundary in its own right across BOTH conversions out of the suppression window, so a running observation captured before it cannot re-arm the session it retired.** Codex B1 verdict (`msg_872d0d03dd6e4a49`, REQUEST_CHANGES on `90cfbd9`, one P2). **The defect.** `suppress_hook_report_with_session_ref` (`src/terminal/state.rs`) seeds `last_loss_observed_at` only for a `ProcessExit` retirement, and rightly so: an API clear or release observes no process at all. The consequence was that for a release the ONLY timestamp the retirement left behind was `SuppressedHookReport::observed_at`, and `retire_suppressed_session_after_accepting` forwarded just the loss to `remember_stale_hook_session` and dropped it — so the stale session created when a NEW session is accepted carried no boundary whatsoever. Reachable through the production state methods: a Hermes session A is started and a running observation is captured; A is released; startup of a new session B is accepted (the fresh-session rule allows it, and that acceptance consumes the suppression and converts A into a stale session); the PRE-release running observation is then delivered and reaches the already-stale re-arm loop in `clear_hook_suppression_for_detected_agent`, which arms A on evidence older than A's own release; an explicit `resume` naming A is then admitted with zero post-release process evidence and repoints the pane's hook and session anchor from B back to A. The suppression's own `detected_state_observed_before_release_suppression` gate did not cover this, because it reads `suppressed_hook_reports` and the acceptance had already removed the entry. The other conversion dropped the same timestamp for the same reason, so `clear_hook_suppression_for_detected_agent` handed its own converted session an empty boundary too. **Fix — the retirement is a fact in its own right, exactly as the loss already is.** `StaleHookSession` gains `retired_at: Instant` beside `last_loss_observed_at`, non-optional because a stale session exists only because of a retirement. A new `evidence_boundary` returns the LATER of the two, and it is the single bar both sides read: `record_fresh_process_evidence` refuses any observation not strictly newer than it (in place of the loss-only check), and `has_reclaimable_process_evidence` re-checks the same relation at the reclaim so the two sides cannot drift apart. A new `observe_retirement` keeps the NEWEST retirement — the same keep-the-newest rule `observe_process_loss` applies to a loss, so a reordered older retirement cannot roll the boundary back — and drops the evidence that retirement re-scopes; `suppress_hook_report_with_session_ref` calls it where it previously only wrote `fresh_process_evidence = None`, so a REPEATED retirement now moves the boundary instead of merely clearing a flag whose absence the next observation read as permission. Both conversions carry `SuppressedHookReport::observed_at` through: `retire_suppressed_session_after_accepting` and `clear_hook_suppression_for_detected_agent`, into a `remember_stale_hook_session` that applies the retirement first, then the loss, then the evidence, all through `StaleHookSession`'s own accessors — so the very observation performing a conversion is gated by the boundary it inherits. **Not weakened.** The legitimate resume still passes: an observation genuinely newer than every retirement and loss re-arms the session and the explicit same-session resume is admitted, which the reviewer's own control probe and the tail of the repeated-release test assert directly. `explicit_session_start_reclaims_stale_session` still demands both limbs — a session-start reason this owner starts on AND fresh evidence; nothing reads `effective_agent_label` or `detected_agent` for identity; handler time still stamps a retirement only where there is no observation at all; and a `ProcessExit` retirement is unchanged in effect, since it seeds both halves with the same instant and the maximum is the boundary it already had. **Tests.** Four new in `src/terminal/state.rs`, three of them RED on `90cfbd9`. The reviewer's two probes (`codex-b1-retirement-conversion-90cfbd9.patch`), renamed without the `review_` prefix and sharing the parameterised body `reclaim_after_new_session_consumes_suppression`: `pre_release_observation_stays_stale_after_new_session_report` (RED — "a pre-release running observation re-armed a retired session after a new-session report consumed its suppression") and the control `post_release_observation_allows_resume_after_new_session_report` (green before and after, so the fix cannot degenerate into a ban). Plus `observation_at_the_release_instant_stays_stale_after_new_session_report`, which pins the equality case by reading the release's own recorded instant back out of the suppression and replaying it as a capture time; and `repeated_releases_keep_the_latest_retirement_boundary`, which retires twice with a new session accepted after each, replays an observation from strictly between the two releases against both retired sessions, and then admits a resume on an observation newer than both — the case a boundary that only cleared the flag cannot express. **No `tests/zynk_receipt.rs` sibling.** The finding turns on the capture timestamp of a detection observation relative to a release, and the CLI/API surface has no way to stamp an observation's capture time — the receipt tests drive a real detector — so no deterministic API-level sibling exists; the new tests drive the production state methods (`set_agent_session_ref_for_session_start`, `release_agent_with_mutation`, `set_detected_state_with_screen_signals_at`) end to end instead. Every existing identity, evidence and retirement control stays green unchanged, including both `reordered_running_observation_cannot_undo_*`, both `queued_running_observation_*`, `process_loss_while_suppressed_fences_an_older_running_observation`, `equal_time_process_loss_invalidates_resume_evidence`, both same-session resume positives, all three retirement negatives and both stale-session generation tests. Verified in an isolated runtime and target: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo build --locked`, `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings`, `terminal::state`+`app::` 732/732 (728 before), `--test zynk_receipt --test zynk_native_cli` 44/44 twice, full `--bin zynk` 2631/2631 (2627 before). `NOTICE` unchanged (fork-authored, no upstream commit). |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only docs correction)* | **`CONTRIBUTING.md` no longer says that Apache-2.0 section 4(b) requires the centralized `NOTICE` enumeration.** Codex B1 nonblocking precision note (`msg_872d0d03dd6e4a49`). The passage read "add that file to the *Modified files (Apache-2.0 provenance)* list in `NOTICE`; Apache-2.0 section 4(b) requires it", which attributes the wrong duty to the clause: §4(b) requires the MODIFIED FILES THEMSELVES to carry prominent notices stating that they were changed. The central index in `NOTICE` is this repository's convention for tracking which files those are — it supports that duty and is not compliance proof by itself. The corrected passage states the clause's actual requirement first, then keeps the repository rule as the convention it is. Docs only: no license, license text or `NOTICE` content changes, and the contribution requirement is unchanged. `python3 -m unittest scripts.test_license_docs` stays green (the guard pins the relicense commit `cd5ea1be`, the Apache provenance and the absence of the retired same-license-as-upstream claim, all preserved). **Observed, not changed:** the same framing appears in `NOTICE` ("Per Apache-2.0 section 4(b): every file … is … enumerated here"), `README.md` ("the Apache-2.0 section 4(b) list of modified files") and `docs/zynk/SPEC.md` ("§4(b) modified-file list in `NOTICE`"); those are outside this correction's scope and are recorded here so a later pass can align them. `NOTICE` unchanged (fork-authored, no upstream commit). |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only test on top of the `e80cfa4` row above)* | **A regression test pinning the retirement boundary on an ALREADY-STALE session, with the loss half held deliberately older so only the retirement can refuse.** Codex B1 precision (`msg_ec7ace170dd16799`) on `e80cfa4`: the boundary must be recorded on sessions that are already stale, not only on the one a conversion is creating, so a repeated retirement can neither lose nor roll back the latest boundary. The production behaviour is already that — every retirement funnels through `suppress_hook_report_with_session_ref`, whose already-stale loop calls `StaleHookSession::observe_retirement`, and that method only ever advances (`if self.retired_at < observed_at`), the same keep-the-newest rule `observe_process_loss` applies — but the row above pinned it only through `repeated_releases_keep_the_latest_retirement_boundary`, where both retirements are releases. **The new test** `a_release_advances_the_boundary_of_a_session_already_stale_from_an_exit` pulls the two halves of the boundary apart. The first session is retired by an observed process EXIT, which seeds the loss boundary and is the ONLY loss this owner ever shows; the process is seen running again, converting the suppression into a stale session and arming it; a second session is accepted, then RELEASED, which observes no process and therefore seeds no loss at all; a third session is accepted so the release's suppression is consumed and a delayed observation can reach the already-stale re-arm loop. Replaying the very observation that armed the first session — newer than the single loss, older than the release — must not re-arm it, and the explicit resume of that session must stay refused with the live session still anchored; an observation newer than the release then re-arms it and the resume is admitted. Nothing but the release recorded on the already-stale entry can produce that refusal, so the test fails on `90cfbd9` with "a release did not advance the boundary of a session that was already stale" and passes here. **Also in this commit:** a missing blank line between `StaleHookSession::observe_retirement` and `record_fresh_process_evidence`, introduced by the row above and invisible to `cargo fmt`. No production behaviour change. Verified in an isolated runtime and target: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo build --locked`, `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings`, `terminal::state`+`app::` 733/733, `--test zynk_receipt --test zynk_native_cli` 44/44 twice, full `--bin zynk` 2632/2632. `NOTICE` unchanged (fork-authored, no upstream commit). |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only fix on top of the `445e7ef` row above)* | **An OLDER, reordered retirement no longer erases NEWER proof that a retired owner's process is alive.** Codex B1 finding (`msg_2f7b62540bcb70d5`, P2): `StaleHookSession::observe_retirement` advanced `retired_at` under the keep-the-newest rule but emptied `fresh_process_evidence` UNCONDITIONALLY. Its already-stale caller is the loop in `suppress_hook_report_with_session_ref`, which runs on every retirement of a REPLACEMENT identity of the SAME owner, so a retirement stamped in the past — the detector's capture time, which the `26e83e3` row made authoritative precisely because handler delay is an ordinary execution mode — discarded evidence strictly newer than BOTH boundaries that retirement left behind. It also made the capture-time rule turn on whether a replacement identity happened to be installed at all: with none, the same observation reaches only `observe_process_loss`, which is timestamp-aware and preserves that very evidence. **The fix** mirrors `observe_process_loss` exactly. `retired_at` still advances as before, and the evidence is then expired only when the RESULTING boundary outdates it: `if self.fresh_process_evidence.is_some_and(|recorded_at| recorded_at <= self.evidence_boundary()) { self.fresh_process_evidence = None; }`. The production diff is that method and its doc comment; no new state, no new field, no other call site. **Nothing is weakened.** The boundary is unchanged and still STRICT, still the LATEST of retirement and loss (`evidence_boundary`), and `has_reclaimable_process_evidence` re-checks the same strict rule at the reclaim: an API clear or release still invalidates evidence captured before or exactly AT it, an equal or newer loss or retirement still invalidates, and the only evidence newly preserved is evidence that already satisfied the strict rule against every boundary held. **Tests** (3 new, all in `src/terminal/state.rs` `mod tests`): `older_exit_retiring_a_replacement_identity_preserves_newer_evidence` and `newer_exit_retiring_a_replacement_identity_expires_older_evidence` share the helper `replayed_exit_with_a_replacement_identity`, which releases session A, accepts replacement B, arms A with a running observation at `base+5ms`, then hands the state machine B's exit captured at `base+3ms` (older, reordered) or `base+6ms` (newer, the control) — every instant is stamped from `base` and then slept past, so each one is already in the PAST when handled and no future stand-in timestamp is used. The older case FAILS on `445e7ef` with the assertion message "a replayed older exit must not erase newer running evidence merely because a replacement identity is installed" (`left: false`, `right: true`) and passes here; the newer case is green on both. `clear_and_release_retirements_keep_strict_boundaries` is the 12-case matrix — clear/release × one/two retirements × an observation earlier than, exactly at, and newer than the last retirement — pinning that a resume is admitted if and only if the observation is strictly newer; green before and after, so it is the guard that the fix relaxed nothing. **Scope note:** Codex's probe asserted the defect through an explicit resume, and that assertion is unreachable on this tree, so the tests assert the invariant on the stale session instead. Retiring the replacement identity on an observed exit also opens a `ProcessExit` suppression, and `hook_report_is_suppressed` refuses EVERY report from a suppressed owner on that reason alone, before the stale-session reclaim is consulted; `detected_state_observed_before_release_suppression` then ignores any observation captured at or before a suppression, so the only observation that could lift that wall is strictly newer than the retirement instant — which is exactly an observation that re-arms the evidence by itself, leaving the two behaviours indistinguishable end to end. The helper therefore asserts `fresh_process_evidence` and `has_reclaimable_process_evidence` on the stale session, and pins the wall with a refusal assertion whose comment records why it is the suppression window and not the freshness rule that refuses. Verified in an isolated runtime and target: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean, `terminal::state`+`app::` 719/719 (716 at `445e7ef`), `--test zynk_receipt --test zynk_native_cli` 44/44 twice, full `--bin zynk` 2635/2635 (2632 at `445e7ef`). `NOTICE` unchanged (fork-authored, no upstream commit). |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only test determinism on top of the `6d376a9` row above)* | **Several `terminal::state` tests now derive their observation instants from the stamp the production code ACTUALLY recorded, instead of a baseline captured before the setup ran.** Test-only: no production line changes. The pattern was `let now = Instant::now();` at the top of a test, then a setup call that stamps `Instant::now()` INTERNALLY (`set_hook_authority`, `set_hook_authority_with_custom_status`, `set_hook_authority_with_session_ref`, `record_identity_only_hook_report`, `set_agent_session_ref_for_session_start` for a session-identity-only owner, `release_agent`, `clear_hook_authority`), then an observation handed to an `_at` method at `now + k` for a small `k`. Every ordering rule in this file compares the observation against that RECORDED stamp — `hook_authority_not_newer_than`, `hook_identity_not_newer_than`, `detected_state_observed_before_release_suppression` and the `StaleHookSession` boundaries — so whenever the setup outran `k` the observation was captured OLDER than the report it was meant to follow, the reordered-observation rule ignored it, and the assertion flipped. `late_full_lifecycle_hook_with_same_session_after_process_exit_does_not_reacquire_authority` was the tightest case: `k = 1 ms` with a `test_session_path` filesystem call and two stamping setters inside the window. An implementer saw it fail once under a saturated runner and reported it to Codex as a test-only bug. **The fix** adds two test helpers, `authority_reported_at` and `identity_reported_at`, which read `hook_authority.reported_at` / `hook_identity.reported_at` back out of the state after the setup, and re-anchors TEN baselines on them — or, where the test needs an instant strictly BEFORE a retirement, on `suppressed_hook_reports[..].observed_at`, the shape the B1 tests above already use. Every offset, assertion and intended ordering is preserved; no test proves anything different; no `thread::sleep` was added and the three pre-existing ones in this file are untouched. Re-anchored: `late_full_lifecycle_hook_with_same_session_after_process_exit_does_not_reacquire_authority` (authority stamp `+1 ms`), `release_suppression_ignores_same_agent_idle_publish` (`retired_at - 1 ms`), `identity_resumes_after_process_exit_with_fresh_process_evidence`, `same_session_resume_after_retirement`, `reordered_running_observation_after_exit`, `identity_same_session_resume_cannot_use_process_evidence_invalidated_by_a_later_exit`, `identity_same_session_resume_after_an_invalidating_exit_needs_new_process_evidence`, `identity_same_session_resume_keeps_process_evidence_after_an_older_exit_observation`, `same_session_resume_without_fresh_process_evidence` and `identity_late_callback_after_fresh_process_evidence_stays_retired` (identity stamp). The EIGHTEEN remaining baselines in the module were audited and left alone: each either hands its own `now` straight to `set_hook_authority_with_custom_status_at` as the recorded stamp, or is captured AFTER the setter that stamps, so no delay can reorder it. **Forced-delay proof.** On `6d376a9`, `std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(3))` inserted immediately after `let now = Instant::now();` fails the exemplar with `assertion failed: late.is_none()`; the same sleep passes after the re-anchoring. A 1100 ms sleep fails `identity_same_session_resume_after_process_exit_with_fresh_process_evidence` on `retirement dropped nothing` and `identity_resumes_after_process_exit_with_fresh_process_evidence` on `assertion failed: terminal.hook_identity.is_none()`, and both pass after it. `release_suppression_ignores_same_agent_idle_publish` is the one shape no delay can break — a pre-setup baseline is always OLDER than the stamp, which is the direction that test needs — so it is pinned instead by flipping its derived offset to `retired_at + 1 ms`, which fails on `assertion failed: change.effective_state_change.is_none()`: the new anchor sits exactly on the decision boundary, which the old pre-setup baseline could never demonstrate. Every sleep and mutation was removed before committing. **Verified** in an isolated runtime and target: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean, `terminal::state` 123/123 five times consecutively and three more under a 32-way CPU load, `terminal::state`+`app::` 719/719 (719 at `6d376a9`, unchanged), `--test zynk_receipt --test zynk_native_cli` 44/44 twice, full `--bin zynk` 2635/2635. `NOTICE` unchanged (fork-authored, no upstream commit). |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only detection fix on top of the `b70ee25` row above; corrects the M3-09 `0c8eff4` boxed rule)* | **`current_approval_boxed` now proves the footer row is INSIDE the box before it claims a current boxed dialog.** Gate-3 arbiter finding, ARB-18C structural variant (`msg_ff224e77924eac1e`, `msg_805b00322058ccfa`). The M3-09 rule (`0c8eff4`) closed its comment with "Structure, not a wider window", but only HALF of that structure was ever proven: the closing-border regex was anchored with `\z` to the end of the region, while NOTHING tied the hint to the box. `contains = ["esc to cancel"]` is satisfied anywhere in the last TWO logical non-empty lines, so an OLD, already-answered, UNBOXED footer paired with ANY border-SHAPED trailing row — a tool-result box bottom, a nested box, a divider — matched at 990, and a working Claude sat on Blocked with `visible_blocker`. The arbiter reproduced it on the production snapshot path at 80/40/30/20 columns × both busy titles (`probe-detection-border.log`). **Fix — manifest only, no engine change:** `src/detect/manifests/claude.toml:43-46` gains a first regex `(?im)^[ \t]*[│┃║][^\n]*esc to cancel[^\n]*[│┃║][ \t]*$` ahead of the unchanged anchored closing-border pattern, which stays last. Every listed regex must match (`src/detect/manifest.rs:1228`, in `compiled_gate_matches` at `:1219`) and regexes see the RAW region text — `contains` is the lowercased one — so the new pattern spells its own `(?i)`. Both rows are now proven box chrome: the hint carries a side border on either side of it on its OWN logical line, and the closing border is the last logical line. **Nothing is weakened:** `state`, `priority` (990), `region` (`bottom_logical_non_empty_lines(2)`), `visible_blocker`, `contains`, `any` and the closing-border regex are byte-identical; adding a required regex can only narrow the rule. The unboxed CURRENT footer keeps its own rule `current_approval_footer` (990, `bottom_logical_non_empty_lines(1)`), so no waiting dialog loses its blocker. `MANIFEST_ENGINE_VERSION` stays 3 (`src/detect/manifest_update.rs:15`); `claude.toml` `version` `2026.09.09.2` -> `2026.09.09.3`. **Tests, RED first:** `pane::terminal::tests::claude_unboxed_answer_with_a_border_shaped_work_row_is_working_at_every_width` renders an answered UNBOXED dialog plus exactly one border-shaped work row — plain `╰──╯` and padded `  ╰──╯  ` — through the real Ghostty terminal at 80/40/30/20 columns × both busy titles and requires Working / `osc_title_working` (RED at `b70ee25`: `left: Blocked` / `current_approval_boxed`); its BOXED twin `claude_boxed_answer_with_a_border_shaped_work_row_is_working_at_every_width` is the control that the fix is structural rather than a hint count (green both before and after). `detect::manifest::tests::claude_boxed_approval_needs_the_footer_row_inside_the_box` pins the four region-level cases: an unboxed footer above a border-shaped row and a HALF-DRAWN footer row with a left border only are Working (both RED at `b70ee25`), while the boxed footer row and the wrapped boxed logical form stay `current_approval_boxed`. | *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-authored fix on top of the row above; `src/kitty_graphics.rs` is upstream `4eceb75d` (pre-relicense) code)* | **A host image whose last visible source disappeared is now released at the end of the frame.** Gate-3 arbiter finding (`msg_2457cb6dc6e8df0e`). `encode_graphics_update` (`src/kitty_graphics.rs:267`) freed a host image on exactly one path — *supersede*, a source that moved to a different host id (`4eceb75d`), deferred to the end of the pass by `4a71ece`. A source that simply stopped being drawn supersedes nothing: the pre-loop prune (`:281`) dropped its `sources` entry and the per-placement loop never saw it, so its upload stayed resident in the terminal for the rest of the process — one leaked image per image a pane ever stopped showing. The tracked test `stale_placement_deletes_placement_not_image_immediately` (`:1383`) asserted the leak itself (`!delete.contains("d=I")` with `images.len() == 1` after an empty frame). Upstream reaches the opposite conclusion through the end-of-frame `dead_sources` sweep in `encode_graphics_update_incremental` (`v0.8.2:src/kitty_graphics.rs`), which deletes a terminal image once its last reference goes; the pane-layer / `HostSourceKey` machinery that sweep is built on is M8's `88370e15` and is **not** ported here. **Fix:** after the stale-placement deletes, the pass collects every key of `host_images` that no value of `sources` references, sorts the ids so emission is deterministic, and releases each through the shared helper (`:407-421`). `release_superseded_image` becomes `release_unreferenced_image` (`:428`) and now serves both callers — same body, parameter `previous` renamed `host_id`: it still refuses to free an image a live source references, still no-ops when an earlier release in the same frame already removed it (one `a=d,d=I` per image per frame), and still drops that image's `host_placements` entries because the host-side `d=I` deletes its placements too. **Nothing is weakened.** The `4a71ece` guarantee is intact: the sweep runs after the whole per-placement loop, so an image that a later source of the same frame adopts is registered in `sources` before anything can free it — nothing is freed mid-loop. The sweep is seated *after* the stale-placement deletes, so a view change keeps tearing stale placements down explicitly (`a=d,d=i`) rather than only implying it through the image delete, and `view_change_deletes_stale_placement_immediately` stays green unchanged. Dedup is untouched: an image two sources share is still referenced by both. **Tests, RED first:** `stale_sole_source_releases_unreferenced_host_image` — one placement, then an empty frame — requires `a=d,d=I,i=<host_id>` with `images`, `placements` and `sources` all empty (RED at `f420480`: *the last reference went away, so the host image is deleted*). `stale_placement_deletes_placement_not_image_immediately` is rewritten as `stale_placement_deletes_placement_and_the_now_unreferenced_image`, asserting both the placement delete and the image release in place of its old leak assertion (RED at `f420480`: *the image no source references any more is released*). `shared_host_image_survives_when_one_source_disappears` is the guard on the other side — two sources dedup to one host image, one stops being drawn, only its placement may go — green before and after by construction, and proved load-bearing by mutation: replacing the "referenced by no source" filter with the naive "release the host image of every pruned source" fails it (`!update.contains("d=I")`) together with four existing dedup/supersede tests. `new_source_keeps_image_needed_later_in_the_same_update`, `new_source_order_does_not_change_superseded_image_handling`, `stale_source_entry_does_not_block_superseded_image_delete`, `shared_host_image_survives_while_another_source_references_it`, `replaced_image_content_deletes_superseded_host_image` and `view_change_deletes_stale_placement_immediately` stay green. **Verification** (isolated target and runtime): `kitty_graphics::` 15 -> 17 tests, all passing; full `--bin zynk` 2640 run / 2640 passed; `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean. **Fork-authored** — no upstream code is imported and `src/kitty_graphics.rs` is pre-relicense (`4eceb75d`), so `NOTICE` gains no entry and is unchanged. |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-authored fix on top of the row above; `src/kitty_graphics.rs` is upstream `4eceb75d` (pre-relicense) code)* | **A host image id that collides with a live one is probed to a free id instead of evicting it.** Gate-3 arbiter finding (`msg_67eb59172715733b`). `host_image_id_for_signature` truncates a 64-bit `DefaultHasher` value into a 900_000-wide namespace (`src/kitty_graphics.rs:609`) and that id is the *cache key* for `host_images`, so two distinct co-visible signatures can claim one id. The second one then took the `Some(_)` "different signature at this id" arm of `encode_graphics_update`: it deleted the resident image (`a=d,d=I`), dropped its `host_placements` entries **and** their `current_placements` membership, and re-uploaded in its slot — so the first source's image vanished from the terminal, its just-emitted placement was then swept as stale in the same frame, and the two sources fought over the slot for as long as both stayed visible. **Fix:** the hash becomes the *starting point*, not the answer. New `resolve_host_image_id(pane_id, signature, host_images) -> Option<u32>` (`:594`) probes forward from the hashed id inside `HOST_IMAGE_ID_BASE..HOST_IMAGE_ID_BASE + HOST_IMAGE_ID_SPAN` (wrapping, at most `HOST_IMAGE_ID_SPAN` steps), stepping past every slot holding *different* content and stopping at the first free slot or at one already holding this exact signature. The `900_000` literal becomes `HOST_IMAGE_ID_SPAN` (`:20`); `host_placement_id` keeps its own literal because it is a different namespace. `None` (the whole namespace occupied by other content) skips that placement with a `tracing::warn!` (`:303-312`) — never an overwrite. With the resolved id the collision arm is unreachable, so the `match` collapses to a single `if host_images.get(&host_id) != Some(&image_signature)` upload branch (`:324`), with a comment recording why the removed arm cannot be reached. `collect_visible_placements` resolves the same way when it decides whether a placement must carry its payload (`:558`), so a signature parked at a probed id still counts as uploaded. **Nothing is weakened.** Content dedup is unchanged: the probe *stops* on an equal signature, so two sources showing one image still share one host upload and one `a=t`. The `sources` supersede bookkeeping and the end-of-frame release from the row above operate on the resolved id unchanged, and neither the `4a71ece` deferral nor the release order moves. The payload decision stays fail-safe in both directions: `host_images` only ever *gains* entries inside the per-placement loop (an id is filled only when `resolve` reported it free, and every release is deferred to after the loop), so a probe that reached a signature in the pre-frame snapshot reaches it again at encode time — data is never omitted for an upload that has to happen. **Observed, not changed:** `host_placement_id` (`:616`) truncates the same way into `1..900_001`; a collision there needs the *same* host image plus two placements whose `(pane, image_id, placement_id)` hashes collide, and it costs one placement's geometry rather than a live image, so it is recorded here rather than fixed. **Tests, RED first** (`DefaultHasher` output is not stable across Rust versions, so the test helper `colliding_data_fingerprints` *searches* for a colliding pair at run time — a birthday search over `data_fingerprint`, ~10^3 candidates — instead of hard-coding one; `hashed_host_image_id` replaces the deleted production helper `host_image_id` at the five test call sites that used it): `host_image_id_collision_preserves_both_visible_placements` encodes one frame with two colliding, distinct, simultaneously visible signatures and requires two uploads at two different ids, both placements cached and no `d=I` (RED at `87c18c4`: *a colliding id must not delete the other source's live image*, with `images.len() == 1` and `placements.len() == 1`). `probing_keeps_the_superseded_release_correct` moves one source onto content that hashes onto the id its own old content still holds, and requires the new image at a *probed* id with the old one released through the superseded path (RED at `87c18c4`: `assertion left != right failed: the new content lives at a probed id, not on top of the old one`, `left: 625023 right: 625023`). `colliding_signature_dedups_only_identical_content` is the dedup guard — two sources, identical content, exactly one `a=t` and one host image — green before and after by design. All 17 earlier `kitty_graphics::` tests stay green. **Verification** (isolated target and runtime): `kitty_graphics::` 17 -> 20 tests, all passing; full `--bin zynk` 2643 run / 2643 passed; `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean; `just gate` clean. **Fork-authored** — no upstream code is imported and `src/kitty_graphics.rs` is pre-relicense (`4eceb75d`), so `NOTICE` gains no entry and is unchanged. |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only identity fix on top of the `b70ee25` row above)* | **A retirement is converted back into a reclaimable session by any running observation newer than its boundaries, not only by a CHANGE of detected agent.** Gate-3 arbiter finding (`msg_fbcdf59a01d6f70d`, confirming the architect helper's probe). `clear_hook_suppression_for_detected_agent` (`src/terminal/state.rs:1230`) is the ONLY path that turns a retirement (`suppressed_hook_reports`) into a `StaleHookSession` carrying fresh process evidence, and the only path that re-arms an already-stale one; its caller `set_detected_state_with_screen_signals_at` (`:416`) runs it on every non-exit observation. It returned early whenever `previous_detected_agent == Some(detected_agent)`, so the conversion never ran for the shape production actually produces: an agent RESTARTED IN PLACE is observed under the same label across its own exit — the screen still shows it, and the detector's miss-confirmation window may never report the agent gone — so a Hermes session retired by an exit at t+1 ms and seen running again at t+2 ms stayed suppressed forever and the agent's own explicit same-session resume was refused until some unrelated label change, withholding receipt authority from a live owner. Every B1 case so far inserted an artificial `None` observation, which is precisely the assumption that does not hold in the field. **Fix:** the label-change early return is gone (`previous_detected_agent` leaves the signature; the caller still uses it for the authority-conflict rule at `:477`), and the trigger becomes the boundary rule the rest of B1 already uses, applied INSIDE the function so it holds however the caller reaches it (`:1241-1246`): a suppression is lifted only by an observation captured strictly after BOTH `suppressed.observed_at` and `suppressed.last_loss_observed_at` when present. The `hook_report_sequences` reset is now gated on at least one suppression actually having been cleared (`:1296`), so a restarted process gets its fresh sequence window exactly once, at the conversion, instead of an open replay window for as long as its agent is detected at all. **Nothing is weakened.** The time gate is strictly-newer on both halves, the same bar `StaleHookSession::record_fresh_process_evidence` applies, so a queued observation captured before its exit still cannot lift the retirement it predates (`queued_running_observation_older_than_its_exit_stays_retired`), a loss seen while the owner was only suppressed still fences an older running observation, and the caller's own `detected_state_observed_before_release_suppression` fence (`:1395`) is untouched. The already-stale re-arm loop is unchanged: it is idempotent and fenced by `record_fresh_process_evidence`, so running it on every observation is correct and cheap. Reclaim still requires the explicit session-start shape — an ordinary late callback is refused however fresh the process is. **Tests, RED first:** `same_label_running_observation_after_an_exit_re_arms_the_retired_session` — exit and running observation under one label, no `None` between — asserts the suppression is converted, the session is armed and the explicit resume is admitted (RED at `6e8209f`: *a running observation newer than the exit left the owner suppressed*). `same_label_running_observation_not_newer_than_the_exit_does_not_lift_it` holds the same shape at the exit's own instant and a millisecond before it — suppression intact, resume refused — then admits a capture 1 ms newer (RED at `6e8209f`: *a running observation genuinely newer than the exit was refused*). `sequence_fence_is_reset_only_when_a_suppression_is_cleared` pins the reset to the conversion: the fence survives an observation with nothing retired, the restarted owner re-anchors at a LOWER sequence once, and a further observation neither reopens the window nor lets a lower-sequence report through (RED at `6e8209f`: *the restarted process could not re-anchor after its retirement was converted*; it also fails against the naive fix that keeps the reset ungated). The App-level twin `queued_state_events_re_arm_an_agent_restarted_in_place` (`src/app/actions.rs`) drives the same two `AppEvent::StateChanged` observations plus the resume through the real dispatch (RED at `6e8209f`). Four test comments that cited the removed label-change rule as their reason are corrected. **Verification** (isolated target and runtime): `terminal::state::tests` + `app::` 719 -> 723 run / 723 passed; `--test zynk_receipt --test zynk_native_cli` 44 passed twice; full `--bin zynk` 2643 -> 2647 run / 2647 passed, 1 skipped; `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean; `just gate` clean. **Fork-only** — `src/terminal/state.rs` identity machinery is fork-authored, so `NOTICE` gains no entry and is unchanged. |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only identity fix on top of the same-label row above)* | **A hook identity accepted between an exit's CAPTURE and its handling is held PROVISIONAL until the detector sees the process again, and anchors no receipt until then.** Gate-3 arbiter finding (`msg_c76820d29bbb759b`, the architect's causal-order gap). Hook reports carry no capture time, so `HookAuthority::reported_at` / `HookIdentity::reported_at` are stamped at ARRIVAL. An exit the detector captured at t0 but the App handled later was compared against that stamp by `hook_authority_not_newer_than` / `hook_identity_not_newer_than` (`src/terminal/state.rs:764-775`, used by the exit path), so a report that ARRIVED at t1 > t0 looked newer than the exit: the late exit retired nothing, and the identity plus its persisted session stayed live and receipt-capable through `authoritative_receiver_identity` (`src/app/creation.rs:346`) although the detector had seen the process gone at t0 and had not seen it since. Retiring outright is wrong the other way: that same window is exactly where a genuinely restarted agent's first `SessionStart` lands, and retiring it would strand the new process, whose later ordinary reports would hit the retirement needing a session-start reason they will never send again. The detector, not the hook, is the process oracle in this fork. **Design:** `HookAuthority` and `HookIdentity` gain `unconfirmed_since: Option<Instant>` (`:41`, `:60`) — the capture instant of an exit this identity must be proven to postdate; `None` is today's confirmed state. The exit path becomes three-way for the owner's own agent (`:447-511`): `reported_at <= now` retires exactly as before; an owner already holding an unanswered exit this one postdates (`hook_owner_has_unanswered_exit_older_than`, `:881`) is retired NOW as `ProcessExit` stamped with THIS exit's capture time, through the existing suppression funnel so late callbacks are fenced as usual; otherwise `hold_hook_owner_unconfirmed` (`:860`) records the pending exit, keeping the OLDEST when one is already pending. `confirm_pending_hook_owner` (`:890`) clears the flag on a RUNNING observation of the pending owner's own agent captured strictly after it, and runs FIRST in `set_detected_state_with_screen_signals_at` (`:404`), before both early returns, so a live full-lifecycle authority or a capture older than a release suppression cannot swallow the confirmation. A new identity recorded while an exit is unanswered INHERITS it (`unanswered_exit_to_inherit`, `:842`, oldest wins): a report is not process evidence, so a replacement identity — including a same-owner session start — starts provisional too. **Receipt gate:** new `TerminalState::confirmed_hook_owner()` (`:821`) returns `(source, agent_label)` only while the live owner is confirmed, keeping the receipt path's own precedence (authority first, identity only when no authority is installed) and never falling through from a pending authority to the other representation; `authoritative_receiver_identity` (`src/app/creation.rs:359`) is its single production consumer, so `zynk.message_received` (`src/app/api/zynk.rs:30`) answers `receiver_identity_unverified` exactly as for a pane no hook ever named. **Every other receipt/awareness consumer was checked:** `src/zynk/receipt.rs` and `src/zynk/receipt_worker.rs` only ever see the `AuthoritativeReceiver` that gate builds; the ADR 0009 awareness header decides its agent target from the pane-info `Party` (`src/zynk/header.rs:383`) and `who` reads `agent_session` off `agent.list` (`src/cli/native.rs:657`) — both go through `terminal_agent_session_info`, which is deliberately UNCHANGED, because the identity still exists and `pane.get` must keep showing the session. `PaneInfo`/`AgentSessionInfo` carry no field for such a flag, so the wire shape is left alone. **Nothing is weakened.** Outside the reorder window every retirement is byte-for-byte what it was; the identity CONFLICT limb (a different agent detected in this owner's place) keeps the ordering rule unchanged, because a contradicting label is not an unanswered question about a process; an API clear or release still retires a pending owner outright; and a provisional identity keeps its persisted session (the session is part of the identity) but cannot anchor a receipt. **Tests, RED first:** `exit_captured_before_a_delayed_hook_report_holds_the_identity_provisional` — the arbiter's shape, a report stamped after an earlier-captured exit — asserts the identity survives, carries `unconfirmed_since == exit_at`, keeps its session and yields no confirmed owner, then flips to confirmed on a running observation 5 ms after the exit (RED: *the unhandled exit was not recorded on the identity it could not retire*, `left: None`). `a_second_exit_while_provisional_retires_the_identity` — two exits with nothing running between — asserts a `ProcessExit` suppression stamped with the SECOND exit, the session dropped, an ordinary same-session report refused, and an explicit resume admitted after fresh evidence. `a_hook_report_not_newer_than_the_exit_is_retired_as_before` is the control, green before and after. `authoritative_receiver_identity_is_none_while_the_hook_identity_is_provisional` (new `src/app/creation.rs` tests module) drives an `App` through the same sequence and requires the receipt anchor to go Some -> None -> Some (RED: *a provisional identity was accepted as a receipt anchor*). `a_hook_report_accepted_across_an_unhandled_exit_is_provisional` (`src/app/actions.rs`) is the App-level twin over the real `HookStateReported` / `StateChanged` dispatch (RED: *an identity accepted across an unhandled exit still anchored a receipt*). **Verification** (isolated target and runtime): `terminal::state::tests` + `app::` 723 -> 728 run / 728 passed; `--test zynk_receipt --test zynk_native_cli` 44 passed twice; full `--bin zynk` 2647 -> 2652 run / 2652 passed, 1 skipped; `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean; `just gate` clean. **Fork-only** — the identity machinery and the receipt gate are fork-authored, so `NOTICE` gains no entry and is unchanged. |
| *(this commit)* | *(none — fork-only identity fix; touches the upstream persist layer at its snapshot/restore seams)* | **The hook RETIREMENT fences now survive a live handoff, so every retirement guarantee the fork makes holds across `server.live_handoff`.** Gate-3 arbiter finding (`msg_24fa384d30dfa9af`). `capture_tab` (`src/persist/snapshot.rs`) built `PaneSnapshot.agent_session` from `terminal.hook_authority` (else `persisted_agent_session`) and NOTHING else of the identity machine, and `restore_handoff` (`src/persist/restore.rs`) re-applied only that through `set_persisted_agent_session`. `hook_report_sequences`, `metadata_report_sequences`, `suppressed_hook_reports` and `stale_hook_sessions` — with their reasons, session refs, `observed_at`, `last_loss_observed_at`, `retired_at` and `fresh_process_evidence` — crossed nothing. Reproduced by the arbiter: a pi session established, released, a receipt for a message addressed to it correctly `receiver_identity_unverified`, then `server.live_handoff`, and an ORDINARY same-session pi report (no session-start reason) was accepted by the new server with no fence to refuse it, so the released session anchored a receipt again. The three retirement negatives, the late-callback refusal and the same-session-resume evidence rule were all void across a handoff. **Design:** a serializable, plain-data `HookRetirementSnapshot` (`src/terminal/state.rs:257`) carries `sequences`, `metadata_sequences`, `suppressed`, `stale`, `hook_identity` and `unanswered_exit`. Instants cannot cross a process, so every one travels as an AGE in whole milliseconds captured against a single `Instant::now()` per pane and is re-based against the restoring server's clock; ties from that granularity always decide against a reclaim, which is the fail-closed direction. `pub fn export_hook_retirement(&self, now: Instant) -> Option<HookRetirementSnapshot>` (`:1074`) returns `None` when nothing is fenced, so an ordinary pane adds no bytes, and sorts every list so a round trip is byte-stable; `pub fn restore_hook_retirement(&mut self, snapshot: HookRetirementSnapshot, now: Instant)` (`:1178`) re-applies everything through the SAME accessors the live paths use — the suppression funnel, then `remember_stale_hook_session` with retirement, loss and evidence in that order — so no invariant can be sidestepped by a restore, and it deliberately re-installs NO `hook_authority`: the owner's next report re-takes it, and it is the fence that must survive. `metadata_report_sequences` IS included: it is the same `seq <= last` replay rule, and a live agent's counter is monotonic across a handoff, so carrying it refuses only genuine replays while dropping it would open a window the old server did not have. The previous commit's provisional state travels too, as `unanswered_exit` — the exit plus the agent it was observed for — held in a new terminal-level `unanswered_hook_exit` field, because the restore installs no authority for a bare age to live on and the next identity the new server records must inherit it. `PaneSnapshot` gains `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] pub hook_retirement: Option<HookRetirementSnapshot>` (`src/persist/snapshot.rs:112`), populated in the capture path; older snapshots and the legacy `RawSessionSnapshot`/`LegacyWorkspaceSnapshot` conversions load unchanged. **Handoff only:** the restore chain gains a `RestoreMode { resume_agents, handoff }` (`src/persist/restore.rs:40`, replacing the `resume_agents_on_restore` boolean so the argument count stays inside `too-many-arguments-threshold = 11`), and `apply_handoff_hook_retirement` (`:454`) applies the field at BOTH seams a restored terminal gets its persisted session at, and only when `handoff` is set. A COLD restore ignores it, and the comment says why: after a full restart the agent processes are gone, every session restarts under the fresh-session rule, and a carried fence could only refuse a legitimate resume. **Ordering:** the handoff capture (`src/server/headless.rs:815`) runs after the DB workers are paused, all clients are disconnected and every terminal's handoff reader is paused, so nothing can mutate `AppState` after the fences are read. **No receipt code changes:** once the fence survives, `authoritative_receiver_identity` refuses the anchor exactly as it did before the handoff. **Tests, RED first** (`tests/live_handoff.rs`, proved by disabling only the restore application and re-running): `live_handoff_keeps_a_released_session_retired` — the arbiter's scenario end to end, probing the gate with `zynk.message_received`, which resolves the hook-authoritative receiver before it looks at the message at all (RED: `left: "socket_namespace_mismatch"`, i.e. the identity resolved and the released session was receipt-capable again). `live_handoff_keeps_the_sequence_fence` — a report behind the pre-handoff sequence must anchor nothing, one ahead of it must be accepted (RED with the same signature). `live_handoff_admits_a_genuinely_new_session` is the control that the carried retirement bans the SESSION, not the owner: green in both states by construction. State layer: `hook_retirement_round_trip_preserves_every_fence` builds a `ProcessExit` retirement with an armed stale session, a release-retired second owner and a live identity, with real 10 ms gaps between the phases, exports, restores 250 ms later into a fresh terminal, and requires the re-export to equal the snapshot, both terminals to decide `hook_report_is_suppressed` / `hook_report_survives_retirement` identically for the same probes, and a post-restore running observation to re-arm exactly as before; `hook_retirement_restore_refuses_evidence_older_than_a_restored_boundary` is the negative — an observation captured before the restored retirement must NOT re-arm, one after it must (it fails against a restore that drops the ages); `hook_retirement_export_is_none_when_nothing_is_retired`. Persist layer: `capture_contract_tracks_hook_retirement`, plus `handoff_restore_carries_the_hook_retirement_fences`, `cold_restore_ignores_the_hook_retirement_fences` and `handoff_restore_accepts_a_snapshot_without_hook_retirement`. **Verification** (isolated target and runtime): `terminal::state::tests` + `app::` 728 -> 731 run / 731 passed; `persist::` 101 -> 105 run / 105 passed; `--test live_handoff` 26 -> 29 run / 29 passed (including `live_server_holds_one_pty_master_fd_per_pane`, the known environment-limited case, which passed here); `--test zynk_receipt --test zynk_native_cli` 44 passed twice; full `--bin zynk` 2652 -> 2659 run / 2659 passed, 1 skipped; `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean; `just gate` clean. **NOTICE unchanged:** `src/persist/snapshot.rs` and `src/persist/restore.rs` are NOT in the `apache-modified-files` index (they carry no post-`cd5ea1be` upstream code), and `src/terminal/state.rs` is already listed, so nothing is added or removed. |
